(which is what is required on the event
\mathcal{E}_{h}
for strong convexity), but
\langle\bm{H},\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\rangle
does concentrate at this rate uniformly over all matrices
\bm{U}
.

We then demonstrate that when
\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\leq R
, then this automatically implies that
\bm{U}\in\mathcal{R}
. Consequently, by strong convexity, we show that there is a high-probability event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf ST}}
(depending only on
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})
) such that if

\displaystyle\frac{\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})\|_{F}}{\sqrt{dr}}>\frac{\varepsilon}{2},

(59)

then
\bm{U}
must satisfy

\displaystyle h(\bm{U})>\min_{\bm{U}:\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}^{*},\bm{U}}\|_{F}\leq\frac{R}{8\|\bm{U}^{*}\|}}h(\bm{U})+C\varepsilon^{2},

(60)

where
C
is some universal constant. The above display turns out to be equivalent to the expression

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}^{(n)}\bigg(\mathbb{B}_{\varepsilon/2}^{c}(\bm{\widehat{W}}/\sqrt{dr})\cap\mathrm{rank}(\bm{W}+\bm{M})\leq r\cap\{\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\leq R\}\bigg)>L_{\lambda}^{*}+C\varepsilon^{2},

(61)

which occurs on the event
\mathcal{E}_{h}\cap\mathcal{E}_{{\sf ST}}
. Therefore, the proof is completed by tabulating the probabilities of these events.
∎

Study of Debiased Estimators

In this section we study the debiased estimators
\bm{Z}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
and
\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
defined via

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{P}_{{\sf rank}-r}\bigg(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(y_{i}-\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}\bigg)\bm{X}_{i}\bigg);

(62)

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}

\displaystyle:=\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\lambda\big(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{-1}\bigg)^{1/2}.

(63)

In
Section

4.1
we prove that both definitions above are equivalent on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
, and then in
Section

4.2
we study their high-dimensional properties.

Equivalence of Debiased Estimators

Similar to the case of
\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}
and
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}
, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
, both
\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
and
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}
are equivalent, which we demonstrate in the following theorem.

Theorem 5
.

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
, under
2
and
3
it holds that

\displaystyle\bm{Z}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=\bm{V}_{U}\big(\bm{\Sigma}+\lambda\bm{I}\big)\bm{V}_{U}^{\top},

(64)

where
\bm{V}_{U}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{U}^{\top}
is the singular value decomposition of
\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}
. Furthermore, on this same event it holds that

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)\top}

\displaystyle=\bm{Z}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}.

(65)

Similar to
Theorem

2
, this result is deterministic on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
.

Proof.

Observe that from
Theorem

2
, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
it holds that
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}=\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}
under
2
and
3
. Furthermore, from the proof of
Theorem

2
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{*}\mathcal{X}\big(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}-\bm{M}\big)-\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}=-\lambda\bm{V}_{U}\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}+\bm{W},

(66)

where
\bm{W}
is orthogonal to
\bm{V}_{U}
and has spectral norm bounded by
\lambda
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(y_{i}-\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}\rangle\bigg)\bm{X}_{i}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}+\mathcal{X}^{*}\mathcal{X}\big(\bm{M}-\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}\big)+\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}

(67)

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}+\lambda\bm{V}_{U}\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}-\bm{W}

(68)

\displaystyle=\bm{V}_{U}\big(\bm{\Sigma}+\lambda\bm{I}\big)\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}-\bm{W},

(69)

where the final equality is due to the fact that
\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}
has singular vectors
\bm{V}_{U}
. After taking the rank
r
projection and recognizing that the singular values of the matrix
\bm{V}_{U}\big(\bm{\Sigma}+\lambda\bm{I}\big)\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}
are strictly larger than
\lambda
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}

\displaystyle=\bm{V}_{U}\big(\bm{\Sigma}+\lambda\bm{I}\big)\bm{V}_{U}^{\top},

(70)

which proves (
64
).
Furthermore, if
\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}=\bm{V}_{U}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{U}^{\top}
, then

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}_{{\sf deb}}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\lambda\big(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{-1}\bigg)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}

(71)

\displaystyle=\bm{V}_{U}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{W}_{U}^{\top}\bigg(\bm{I}+\lambda\bm{W}_{U}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\bm{W}_{U}^{\top}\bigg)\bm{W}_{U}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}

(72)

\displaystyle=\bm{V}_{U}\big(\bm{\Sigma}+\lambda\bm{I}\big)\bm{V}_{U}^{\top},

(73)

which proves (
65
).
∎

Concentration of Debiased Estimators about Hard-Thresholding Estimator

Next, as in the case of the previous section, we will show that both
\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}
and
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}
concentrate around a particular hard-thresholding estimator (that may not necessarily be equal to
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}
if
\gamma_{n}
remains bounded). Define

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf HT}}:=\mathcal{P}_{{\sf rank}-r}\bigg(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}\bigg),

(74)

where
\tau^{*}
denotes the parameter from the fixed-point equations in (
46
). Here we note that
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}
only depends on
\lambda
implicitly through
\tau^{*}
.
The following result shows that
\bm{Z}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
closely approximates
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}
.

Theorem 6

(High-Dimensional Asymptotics for
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf HT}}
)
.

Suppose that
1
,
2
and
3
are satisfied.
Let
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}
denote the debiased convex estimator. Let
\phi
be any
1
-Lipschitz function on
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|

\displaystyle\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}_{\bm{H}}\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{\sf HT}\bigg)\bigg|>\varepsilon\bigg\}

(75)

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\exp(-cd)+\exp(-cdr)+\exp(-cn)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\exp(-cdr\varepsilon^{4})-\exp(-\frac{(dr)^{2}}{n}\varepsilon^{4})\bigg).

(76)

Proof.

We will argue that
\phi\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)
is a Lipschitz function of
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}
on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
. Indeed, on this event, by the proof of
Theorem

2
, it holds that
\|\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{dr}
. From the condition
\lambda_{\min}(\bm{M})\geq C_{0}\sigma\sqrt{dr}
, Weyl’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)})\geq\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})-C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\geq(C_{0}-C)\sigma\sqrt{dr}\geq\lambda\sqrt{r},

(77)

provided that
C_{0}
is sufficiently large. From
Theorem

5
, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
it holds that
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}=\bm{V}_{U}\big(\Sigma+\lambda\bm{I}\big)\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}
, where
\bm{V}_{U}
are the leading
r
eigenvectors of
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}
. Let
\eta
denote the function which takes a matrix and adds
\lambda/\sqrt{dr}
to the leading
r
eigenvalues of a matrix. Then since
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}=\bm{V}_{U}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\eta(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}/\sqrt{dr})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{V}_{U}\big(\bm{\Sigma}+\lambda\bm{I}\big)\bm{V}_{U}^{\top}=\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}/\sqrt{dr},

(78)

so that
\eta(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}/\sqrt{dr})=\bm{Z}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}/\sqrt{dr}
. We claim that
\eta
is Lipschitz on the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}:=\{\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\lambda_{r+1}(\bm{A})=0;\lambda_{r}(\bm{A})\geq\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{d}}\},

(79)

which contains
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}/\sqrt{dr}
on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
.
To see that
\eta
is Lipschitz, suppose that
\bm{A}
and
\bm{B}
are two matrices in
\mathcal{S}
, and let them have eigendecompositions
\bm{U}_{A}\bm{\Sigma}_{A}\bm{U}_{A}^{\top}
and
\bm{U}_{B}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}\bm{U}_{B}^{\top}
respectively. First, if
\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}<\lambda/\sqrt{d}
, then

\displaystyle\|\eta(\bm{A})-\eta(\bm{B})\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{U}_{A}(\bm{\Sigma}+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{I})\bm{U}_{A}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{B}(\bm{\Sigma}_{B}+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{I})\bm{U}_{B}^{\top}\|_{F}

(80)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{dr}}\|\bm{U}_{A}\bm{U}_{A}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{B}\bm{U}_{B}^{\top}\|_{F}

(81)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{dr}}\frac{\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}}{\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{d}}}

(82)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F},

(83)

where we have used the Davis-Kahan Theorem. On the other hand, if
\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}\geq\lambda/\sqrt{d}
, then

\displaystyle\|\eta(\bm{A})-\eta(\bm{B})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{dr}}\|\bm{U}_{A}\bm{U}_{A}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{B}\bm{U}_{B}^{\top}\|_{F}

(84)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F}\|\bm{U}_{A}\bm{U}_{A}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{B}\bm{U}_{B}^{\top}\|_{F}

(85)

\displaystyle\leq 3\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{F},

(86)

since the Frobenius norm of the difference of projection matrices is always at most
\sqrt{2r}
. Consequently,
\eta
is at most a
3
-Lipschitz function on this set. By Kirszbaum’s theorem, there exists a Lipschitz extension
\widetilde{\eta}:\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that
\widetilde{\eta}
has the same Lipschitz constant of
\eta
and
\widetilde{\eta}|_{\mathcal{S}}=\eta
. In particular, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
,
\eta
and
\widetilde{\eta}
coincide. Let
\phi
be any Lipschitz function, and let
\widetilde{\phi}=\phi\circ\widetilde{\eta}
, which is also Lipschitz. By
Theorem

4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\phi\bigg(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})\bigg)>\varepsilon\bigg\}

(87)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})\bigg)>\varepsilon\bigg\}\cap\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}+\mathbb{P}\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}^{c}

(88)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\widetilde{\phi}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\widetilde{\phi}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})\bigg)>\varepsilon\bigg\}+\mathbb{P}\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}^{c}

(89)

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\exp(-cd)+\exp(-cdr)+\exp(-cn)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\exp(-cdr\varepsilon^{4})-\exp(-\frac{(dr)^{2}}{n}\varepsilon^{4})\bigg),

(90)

which completes the proof.
∎

As in the previous steps observe that the rate of concentration does not have an additional error term depending on
\gamma_{n}
, which is due to the fact that
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}\neq\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}
when
\gamma_{n}
is bounded away from infinity, and, moreover, this replacement is non-negligible in such a regime. However, the following lemma shows that
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}
approximates
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}
when
\gamma_{n}\to\infty
, the latter of which does not depend on
\lambda
.

Lemma 8
.

Suppose that
1
,
2
and
3
are satisfied. Then for any
1
-Lipschitz function
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\to\mathbb{R}
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\big(\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}/\sqrt{dr}\big)-\mathbb{E}\phi\big(\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}/\sqrt{dr}\big)\bigg|=O\bigg(\sigma\frac{dr}{n}\bigg).

(91)

Proof.

See
Section

A.4
.
∎

Concentration of Unregularized Estimator about Debiased Estimator

Our final argument is based on demonstrating
\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
approximates
\bm{U}^{(0)}
. The following result establishes the near-equivalence of
\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
and
\bm{U}^{(0)}
. We prove this result in the following subsection.

Theorem 7

(Relating the Debiased and Unregularized Nonconvex Estimators)
.

Suppose that
1
,
2
and
3
are satisfied. Then the debiased estimator
\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}\|_{F}=O\bigg(\sigma r\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\bigg)

(92)

with probability at least
1-O\bigg(\exp(-cdr)+\exp(-cn)+\exp(-cd)\bigg)
.

This step is the only step essentially requiring that
\gamma_{n}/r\to\infty
, since observe that the rate of approximation is of order
(\gamma_{n}/r)^{-1/2}
from
Theorem

7
. In essence, this rate is due to the
the rate of concentration of
\mathcal{X}^{*}\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{I}
on rank
r
matrices. Therefore, in order to replace
\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)\top}
with
\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}
, we incur an error of order
O((\gamma_{n}/r)^{-1/2})
, which is partially where this extra term in
Theorem

1
arises.

Proof of
Theorem

7

In order to prove
Theorem

7
we will require a number of intermediate results. The first result bounds the norms of the gradients of
f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}
when evaluated at
\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
.

Lemma 9
.

Under
1
,
2
and
3
, with probability at least
1-O\bigg(\exp(-cdr)-\exp(-cn)\bigg)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)\|_{F}\lesssim\frac{\lambda r\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\|_{F}.

(93)

Proof.

See
Section

D.1
.
∎

The next result characterizes the landscape of
f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}
about the quantity
\bm{U}^{(0)}
. Without loss of generality we may take
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}^{(0)},\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}}=\bm{I}
, since if
\bm{U}^{(0)}
is a local minimum of
f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}
then so is
\bm{U}^{(0)}\mathcal{O}
for any orthogonal matrix
\mathcal{O}
.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose without loss of generality that

\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}^{(0)},\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}}=\bm{I}
. Under
1
,
2
and
3
, with probability at least
1-O\big(\exp(-cdr)+\exp(-cd)\big)
it holds that

\displaystyle 20\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\nabla^{2}f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(t\bm{U}^{(0)}+(1-t)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)[\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)},\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}]

(94)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{M})}{20}\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\|_{F}^{2}.

(95)

Furthermore, it also holds that

\displaystyle\max\{\|\bm{U}^{(0)}\|^{2},\|\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|^{2}\}\leq 8\|\bm{M}\|

(96)

Proof.

See
Section

D.2
.
∎

The proof of this result bears some similarity to existing works in the literature studying the optimization geometry of nonconvex problems
(
Ge et al. 2017
;
Luo and Trillos 2022
)
. In fact, en route to the proof of this result we actually prove a slightly stronger statement; see
Lemma

24
. Our proof is based on arguing that
\bm{U}(t)=t\bm{U}^{(0)}+(1-t)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}
lies in a geodesic ball of
\bm{U}^{*}
with radius at most
C\sigma\sqrt{dr}
with high probability; strong convexity and smoothness follows.

Proof of
Theorem

7
.

First, since
\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(0)})
vanishes, for any
\alpha>0
it holds that

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{\sf deb}-\alpha\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}})-\bm{U}^{(0)}+\alpha\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(0)})+\alpha\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}})

(97)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(t\bm{U}^{(0)}+(1-t)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)dt\bigg)\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)+\alpha\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}),

(98)

where the second line follows from the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus, where above we slightly abuse notation by letting
\nabla^{2}f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}
be of appropriate compatible dimension so that the equation above is meaningful (note that no generality is lost by doing so).
Taking norms, we have that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(t\bm{U}^{(0)}+(1-t)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)dt\bigg)\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)\bigg\|_{F}+\alpha\|\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\big)\|_{F}.

(99)

Observe that

\displaystyle\bigg\|

\displaystyle\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(t\bm{U}^{(0)}+(1-t)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)dt\bigg)\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)\bigg\|_{F}^{2}

(100)

\displaystyle={\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(t\bm{U}^{(0)}+(1-t)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)dt\bigg)^{2}{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg).

(101)

Denote

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}:=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\big(t\bm{U}^{(0)}+(1-t)\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big)dt.

(102)

Let
\alpha=\frac{c_{0}}{20\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})}
. Then by
Lemma

10
it holds that on the event therein,

\displaystyle\alpha{\sf vec}\big(\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\big(\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 20\alpha\lambda_{\max}\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}^{2}

(103)

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}^{2},

(104)

and also

\displaystyle\alpha{\sf vec}\big(\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\big(\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{0}}{400\kappa}\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}^{2}.

(105)

Consequently,

\displaystyle{\sf vec}\big(\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\big)^{\top}\bigg(\bm{I}-\alpha\bm{\Gamma}\bigg){\sf vec}\big(\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{c_{0}}{400\kappa})\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}^{2}.

(106)

As a result,

\displaystyle{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)^{\top}

\displaystyle\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\bm{\Gamma}\bigg)^{2}{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)

(107)

\displaystyle={\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\bm{\Gamma}\bigg){\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)

(108)

\displaystyle\quad-\alpha{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\bm{\Gamma}\bigg)\bm{\Gamma}{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)

(109)

\displaystyle={\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)^{\top}\bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\alpha\bm{\Gamma}\bigg){\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)

(110)

\displaystyle\quad-\alpha{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)

(111)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2}{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}^{2}{\sf vec}\bigg(\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bigg)

(112)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{c_{0}}{400\kappa})\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}^{2}-\frac{c_{0}}{400\kappa}\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}^{2}+c_{0}^{2}\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}^{2}

(113)

\displaystyle=\|\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg(1-\frac{c_{0}}{800\kappa}+c_{0}^{2}\bigg)

(114)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\frac{c_{0}}{1600\kappa})\|\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\|_{F}^{2}.

(115)

as long as
c_{0}\leq\frac{1}{800\kappa}
.
Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\|_{F}\leq(1-\frac{c_{0}}{1600\kappa})\|\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\|_{F}+\alpha\|\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\|_{F}.

(116)

After rearrangement we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1600\kappa}{c_{0}}\alpha\|\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}})\|_{F}=\frac{80}{\kappa\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})}\|\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}})\|_{F},

(117)

By
Lemma

10
, on the event therein,

\displaystyle\max\bigg\{\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\|,\|\bm{U}^{(0)}\|\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})}.

(118)

By
Lemma

9
we have that with the probability therein

\displaystyle\|\nabla f^{(0)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda r\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\|_{F}.

(119)

Therefore, plugging (
119
) and (
118
) into (
117
), it holds that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\kappa\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})}\frac{\lambda r\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{U}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}\|_{F}\lesssim\frac{\sigma r^{3/2}d}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})}\sqrt{n}}.

Therefore, with the probability from
Lemmas

9
and
10
it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\|\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}-\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}_{{\sf deb}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg(\big\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big\|_{F}\big\|\bm{U}^{(0)}\big\|+\big\|\bm{U}^{(0)}-\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big\|_{F}\big\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\big\|\bigg)

(120)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\frac{r^{3/2}d\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})}}{\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{M})}}

(121)

\displaystyle\asymp\sigma\frac{r\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{n}},

(122)

which proves the result after tabulating these probabilities.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

1

We now have all the pieces to prove
Theorem

1
.

Proof of
Theorem

1
.

Let
\phi(\cdot)
be any 1-Lipschitz function. For the convex estimator, we note that the bound holds directly from
Theorem

4
,
Theorem

2
, and
Lemma

7
from the fact that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\big(\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})/\sqrt{dr}\big)-\mathbb{E}\phi\big(\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}/\sqrt{dr}\big)\bigg|

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\sigma\frac{dr^{3/2}}{n}\bigg).

Therefore, we focus on the proof for the nonconvex estimator.

Define the events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:zdeb}}}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\bigg|\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf HT}}\bigg)\bigg|\leq\varepsilon\bigg\}

(123)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:debunreg}}}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\|\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}_{{\sf deb}}-\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}\|_{F}=O\bigg(\frac{\sigma r\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)\bigg\}.

(124)

By
Lemma

8
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\big(\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}/\sqrt{dr}\big)-\mathbb{E}\phi\big(\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}/\sqrt{dr}\big)\bigg|\leq C\sigma\frac{dr}{n}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}.

By
Theorem

5
on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
it holds that
\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}_{{\sf deb}}=\bm{Z}_{{\sf deb}}^{(\lambda)}
. As a result, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:zdeb}}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:debunreg}}}\cap\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf HT}}\bigg)\bigg|+C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}

(125)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}\bigg)-\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bm{U}^{(\lambda)\top}_{{\sf deb}}\bigg)\bigg|+C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}

(126)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf deb}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf HT}}\bigg)\bigg|+C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}

(127)

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma r\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\varepsilon.

(128)

Therefore, by
Theorems

7
,
6
and
1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|\phi\bigg(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{U}^{(0)}\bm{U}^{(0)\top}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}_{\bm{H}}\phi\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}\big)\bigg|>\varepsilon+C\sigma r\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:zdeb}}}^{c}+\mathbb{P}\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:debunreg}}}^{c}+\mathbb{P}\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}^{c}

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\exp(-cd)+\exp(-cdr)+\exp(-cn)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\exp(-cdr\varepsilon^{4})+\exp(-\frac{(dr)^{2}}{n}\varepsilon^{4})\bigg),

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Proofs of Preliminary Results

In this section we prove our key preliminary results that we rely on throughout our analysis.

Proof of Lemma
1

Proof.

We will bound each quantity separately and take a union bound. First, we note that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\bm{X}_{i}
is equal in distribution to a mean-zero random matrix with off-diagonal variance
\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n}
and diagonal variance
\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{n}
. By Corollary 3.9 of
Bandeira and Handel 2016
, we have that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\bm{X}_{i}\bigg\|\leq 8\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}

(129)

with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-cd\big)
.

For the next event, this result follows from Lemma 1 of
Candès and Plan 2011
, where the modification to
{\sf GOE}(d)
is straightforward.

The final term is handled via
\varepsilon
-net. First, let
\bm{A}
be any rank at most
2r
matrix. Observe that
\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}\rangle
is distributed as a mean-zero Gaussian random variable with variance
\|\bm{A}\|_{F}^{2}
. In addition, the vector
\bm{\varepsilon}
is a Gaussian vector with covariance
\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}
. Therefore, letting
A\sim B
note equality in distribution, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}\rangle\sim\frac{\sigma\|\bm{A}\|_{F}}{n}\langle\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2}\rangle

(130)

where
\bm{z}_{1}
and
\bm{z}_{2}
are standard
\mathcal{N}(0,\bm{I}_{n})
random variables. By rotational invariance, note that since
\langle\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2}\rangle=\|\bm{z}_{1}\|\frac{\langle\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2}\rangle}{\|\bm{z}_{1}\|}
, it holds that
\langle\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2}\rangle
equals in distribution to the random variable
\|\bm{z}_{1}\|G
, where
G
is a standard Gaussian random variable. By Lipschitz concentration
\big|\|\bm{z}_{1}\|-\sqrt{n}\big|\leq t_{1}\sqrt{n}
with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cnt_{1}^{2})
. Furthermore,
|G|\leq t_{2}
with probability at least
1-\exp(-t_{2}^{2})
. Therefore, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cnt_{1}^{2})-2\exp(-t_{2}^{2})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma\|\bm{A}\|_{F}}{n}\big|\langle\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2}\rangle\big|\leq\frac{\sigma\|\bm{A}\|_{F}}{n}\big(\sqrt{n}+t_{1}\sqrt{n}\big)t_{2}=\frac{\sigma\|\bm{A}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{n}}\big(1+t_{1}\big)t_{2}.

(131)

Taking
t_{1}=c
and
t_{2}=C_{t_{2}}\sqrt{dr}
shows that with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cn)-2\exp(-C_{t_{2}}^{2}dr)
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}\rangle\bigg|\leq C_{t_{2}}\sigma\|\bm{A}\|_{F}\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}.

(132)

Now let
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}
denote an
\varepsilon
-net for the set of rank at most
2r
matrices with Frobenius norm at most
1
. By Lemma 3.1 of
Candès and Plan 2011
,
|\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}|\leq\bigg(\frac{9}{\varepsilon}\bigg)^{3dr}
. For any fixed
\bm{A}\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}
it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}\rangle\bigg|\leq C_{t_{2}}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}.

(133)

Consequently, the union bound implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{A}\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}\rangle\bigg|\leq C_{t_{2}}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}

(134)

with probability at least

\displaystyle 1-\exp\bigg(3dr\log(9/\varepsilon)-cn\bigg)-\exp\bigg(4dr\log(9/\varepsilon)-C_{t_{2}}^{2}dr\bigg).

(135)

Now denote

\displaystyle M:=\sup_{\bm{A}:\|\bm{A}\|_{F}\leq 1}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}\rangle\bigg|,

(136)

and let
\bm{A}_{0}
denote the maximizer above. Then there is some
\bm{A}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}
such that
\|\bm{A}_{0}-\bm{A}_{\varepsilon}\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon
, and, furthermore, we can write
\bm{A}_{0}-\bm{A}_{\varepsilon}=\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{A}_{2}
, where both
\bm{A}_{1}
and
\bm{A}_{2}
are rank at most
2r
and
\|\bm{A}_{1}\|_{F},\|\bm{A}_{2}\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon
. Then on the event (
134
), it holds that

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}_{0}\rangle\bigg|

(137)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}_{0}-\bm{A}_{\varepsilon}\rangle\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}_{\varepsilon}\rangle\bigg|

(138)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}_{1}+\bm{A}_{2}\rangle\bigg|+C_{t_{2}}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}

(139)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}_{1}\rangle\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{A}_{2}\rangle\bigg|+C_{t_{2}}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}

(140)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon M+C_{t_{2}}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}.

(141)

Rearranging shows that on this same event

\displaystyle M\leq\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}C_{t_{2}}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{dr}{n}}.

(142)

Consequently, by taking
\varepsilon=1/4
, as long as
C_{t_{2}}
is larger than some universal constant, this probability is at least
1-C\exp(-Cdr)
since
n\geq C_{0}dr
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

5

Proof of
Lemma

5
.

To prove existence and uniqueness of
\tau^{*},\zeta^{*}
, we follow the argument of Lemma A.2 of
Celentano et al. 2023
. Define functions
\mathcal{T},\mathcal{Z}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\to\mathbb{R}
via

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}):=\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}^{2}}{n},

(143)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}):=\bigg(1-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}\rangle_{L^{2},F}}{n\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z})}\bigg)_{+}.

(144)

Define the function
\mathcal{E}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\to\mathbb{R}
via

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z}):=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z-M}\|_{L^{2},F}^{2}}{n}}-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z-M}\rangle_{L^{2},F}}{n}\bigg)^{2}_{+}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|\bm{Z}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\},

(145)

where we note that
\bm{Z}
is viewed as a function
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}_{\succcurlyeq 0}
in
L^{2,F}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
.

Step 1: Showing
\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})
has minimizers.

We will show that
\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})\to\infty
as
\|\bm{Z}\|_{L^{2},F}\to\infty
, whence
\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})
has a minimizer.
To wit, we note that

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{F}-2\|\bm{M}\|_{*},

(146)

which holds from the standard inequality
\|\bm{Z}\|_{*}\geq\|\bm{Z}\|_{F}
. In addition, for any
M>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{Z}-\bm{M},\bm{H}\rangle|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{Z}(\bm{H})-\bm{M},\bm{H}\mathbb{I}_{\|\bm{H}\|_{F}>M}\rangle_{L^{2},F}+\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{Z}(\bm{H})-\bm{M},\bm{H}\rangle_{L^{2}_{F}}\mathbb{I}_{\|\bm{H}\|_{F}\leq M}\big|

(147)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}(\mathbb{E}\|\bm{H}\|_{F}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\|\bm{H}\|_{F}>M})^{1/2}+M\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}(\bm{H})-\bm{M}\|_{F}.

(148)

Let
M
be sufficiently large such that
(\mathbb{E}\|\bm{H}\|_{F}^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\|\bm{H}\|_{F}>M})^{1/2}<\sqrt{n}/2.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}}{2\sqrt{n}}-\frac{M}{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}(\bm{H})-\bm{M}\|_{F}\bigg)^{2}_{+}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{F}-2\frac{\lambda}{n}\|\bm{M}\|_{*}.

(149)

We now analyze three cases:

•

Case 1:
\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}/(2\sqrt{n})\leq M\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}(\bm{H})-\bm{M}\|_{F}/n
. In this case, the term inside the parentheses is zero, and it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\geq\frac{\lambda}{2M\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}.

(150)

•

Case 2:
\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}/(4/\sqrt{n})\leq M\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}(\bm{H})-\bm{M}\|_{F}/n<\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}/(2\sqrt{n})
. In this case, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}}{2\sqrt{n}}-\frac{M}{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\bigg)^{2}_{+}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}}{4\sqrt{n}}\bigg)^{2}\geq\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F^{2}}^{2}}{32n}

(151)

•

Case 3:
\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}/(4/\sqrt{n})>M\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}(\bm{H})-\bm{M}\|_{F}/n

. In this case we again have the bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}}{2\sqrt{n}}-\frac{M}{n}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\bigg)^{2}_{+}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}}{4\sqrt{n}}\bigg)^{2}\geq\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F^{2}}^{2}}{32n}.

(152)

Combining these cases, we see that we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})\geq\min\bigg\{\frac{\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}^{2}}{32n},\frac{\lambda}{2m\sqrt{n}}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2},F}-2\frac{\lambda}{n}\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\bigg\}.

(153)

In any case, when
\|\bm{Z}\|_{L^{2},F}\to\infty
, since
\bm{M}
is fixed, it holds that
\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})\to\infty
, whence
\mathcal{E}(\cdot)
has minimizers.

Step 2: Establishing a correspondence between soft-thresholding and minimizers
. Suppose
\bm{Z}^{*}
is any minimizer of
\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})
. Define the following function

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}(\bm{Z},\zeta,\tau):=\frac{\zeta}{2n}\|\bm{Z}-\bm{M}-\tau\bm{H}\|_{L^{2},F}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|\bm{Z}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}.

(154)

Given
\zeta
and
\tau
, it is clear that
\bm{Z}^{*}
minimizes
\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}(\bm{Z},\zeta,\tau)
if and only if
\bm{Z}
satisfies

\displaystyle\bm{Z}(\bm{H})

\displaystyle={\sf S.T.}\big(\bm{M}+\tau\bm{H};\frac{\lambda}{\zeta}\big)

(155)

almost surely. Denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\bm{Z}):=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z-M}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}}-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z-M}\rangle_{L_{2}}}{n}\bigg)^{2}_{+}.

(156)

For fixed
\bm{Z}_{1},\bm{Z}_{2}
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\mathcal{F}\big(\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z}_{1}-\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{n}}-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}-\bm{M}\rangle}{n}\bigg)_{+}

(157)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\big(\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}\big)^{-1/2}\bigg(\frac{2\langle\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle+\varepsilon^{2}\|\bm{Z}_{2}\|^{2}}{n}\bigg)-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}\bigg).

(158)

Evaluating at
\varepsilon=0
yields

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\mathcal{F}(\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2})\bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{n}}-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}\rangle}{n}\bigg]_{+}\bigg[\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{n}\bigg]^{-1/2}\frac{\langle\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}

(159)

\displaystyle\quad-\bigg[\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\frac{\|\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{n}}-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}\rangle}{n}\bigg]_{+}\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}

(160)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1})\frac{\langle\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}-\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}_{1})\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1})\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}

(161)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1})\frac{\langle\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}-\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}_{1})\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}.

(162)

Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}(\bm{Z}_{1})+\varepsilon\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1})\frac{\langle\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}-\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}_{1})\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}+O(\varepsilon^{2}).

(163)

In addition, observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}(\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2},\zeta,\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta}{2n}\|\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}-\bm{M}-\tau\bm{H}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}\|_{*}-\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}

(164)

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta}{2n}\bigg(\|\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}-\tau\bm{H}\|^{2}+2\varepsilon\langle\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}-\tau\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle+\varepsilon^{2}\|\bm{Z}_{2}\|^{2}\bigg)+\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}

(165)

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta}{2n}\|\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}-\tau\bm{H}\|^{2}+\varepsilon\zeta\frac{\langle\bm{Z}_{1}-\bm{M}-\tau\bm{H},\bm{Z}_{2}\rangle}{n}+O(\varepsilon^{2})+\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\|\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}.

(166)

In particular, for
\zeta=\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1})
and
\tau=\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}_{1})
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}(\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2},\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1}),\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}_{1}))-\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z}_{1}+\varepsilon\bm{Z}_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}(\bm{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1}),\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}_{1}))-\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z}_{1})+O(\varepsilon^{2}),

(167)

Consequently,
\bm{Z}_{1}
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z}_{1})
if and only if
\bm{Z}_{1}
is a minimizer of
\bm{Z}_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}(\bm{Z}_{1},\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}_{1}),\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}_{1}))
, which are simply given via

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{*}

\displaystyle={\sf S.T.}\bigg(\bm{M}+\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}^{*})\bm{H};\frac{\lambda}{\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}^{*})}\bigg).

(168)

Since minimizers of
\mathcal{E}(\bm{Z})
exist, it must hold that
\bm{Z}^{*}
exists, and hence solutions to the fixed-point equations exist.

Step 3: Establishing Uniqueness
. Now suppose that
\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}^{*})=0
for some minimizer
\bm{Z}^{*}
. Then
\bm{Z}^{*}=0
almost surely, and hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}^{*})=\big(1-\frac{\langle\bm{H},0\rangle_{L_{2}}}{n\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}^{*})}\big)_{+}=1

(169)

a contradiction. Therefore
\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z}^{*})>0
for all minimizers of
\mathcal{E}
. Finally, the function
\mathcal{E}
is strictly convex on
\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z})>0
. Because all minimizers satisfy
\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z})>0
, restricting
\mathcal{E}
to the set
\mathcal{Z}(\bm{Z})>0
renders
\mathcal{E}
strictly convex and does not eliminate any minimizers, whence the minimizer must be unique by strict convexity.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

6
and Additional Properties of Soft-Thresholding Estimator

In this section we provide some additional characterizations of
\tau^{*}
and
\zeta^{*}
and the soft thresholding estimator
\bm{Z}_{{\sf st}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})
that are useful in subsequent section.

We will also need the following lemma, which concerns the estimator
\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})
.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose
1
,
2
and
3
are satisfied, and let
s>0
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg|>sdr\bigg\}\leq C\exp(-cdr(s\vee s^{2})).

(170)

Moreover,
\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*}))>r\bigg\}\leq C\exp(-cd).
In addition,
\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})\|>2\|\bm{M}\|\bigg\}\leq C\exp(-cd)
.

Proof.

See
Section

A.3.2
.
∎

A.3.1
Proof of
Lemma

6

Proof of
Lemma

6
.

The proof of
Lemma

5
shows that

\displaystyle\zeta^{*}

\displaystyle=\bigg(1-\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}^{*}\rangle_{L_{2}}}{n\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}^{*})}\bigg)_{+}>0

(171)

for any minimizer
\bm{Z}^{*}
of
\mathcal{E}
. This also implies that

\displaystyle\frac{{\sf df}_{\lambda}(\tau^{*2},\zeta^{*})}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n\tau^{*}}\mathbb{E}\bigg\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})\bigg\rangle=\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}^{*}\rangle_{L_{2}}}{n\mathcal{T}(\bm{Z}^{*})}<1,

(172)

which shows that
\zeta^{*}\leq 1
.

We will now demonstrate that
\tau^{2}
is bounded. First, observe that
\tau^{*}
satisfies

\displaystyle(\tau^{*})^{2}=\sigma^{2}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}.

(173)

Since
1\geq\zeta^{*}>0
,
\lambda/\zeta^{*}\geq\lambda
. Therefore, for any fixed
\tau^{*}
, with probability one it holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*}))\leq\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*}).

(174)

Moreover, by
Bandeira and Handel 2016
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\bm{H}\|\leq 3\big(\sqrt{d}+t\big)

(175)

with probability at least
1-\exp(-ct^{2})
. Consequently, by Weyl’s inequality, on this event

\displaystyle|\lambda_{r+1}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})|\leq\tau^{*}\|\bm{H}\|\leq 3\tau^{*}(\sqrt{d}+t).

(176)

Therefore, by
3

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}+2\mathbb{E}\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*}))\|\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*})\|^{2}

(177)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\|\bm{M}\|^{2}+4(\tau^{*})^{2}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*})\|\bm{H}\|^{2}

(178)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\|\bm{M}\|^{2}+4(\tau^{*})^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}\{\lambda_{i}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})>\lambda\}\bigg]\|\bm{H}\|^{2}\bigg\}

(179)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\|\bm{M}\|^{2}+4(\tau^{*})^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}\{\lambda_{i}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})>\lambda\}\bigg]\|\bm{H}\|^{2}\mathbb{I}\{\|\bm{H}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\bigg\}

(180)

\displaystyle\quad++4(\tau^{*})^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}\{\lambda_{i}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})>\lambda\}\bigg]\|\bm{H}\|^{2}\mathbb{I}\{\|\bm{H}\|>C\sqrt{d}\bigg\}

(181)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\|\bm{M}\|^{2}+4(\tau^{*})^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{I}\{\lambda_{i}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})>\lambda\}\bigg]\|\bm{H}\|^{2}\mathbb{I}\{\|\bm{H}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\bigg\}

(182)

\displaystyle\quad+8(\tau^{*})^{2}d\int_{C^{2}d}^{\infty}e^{-cs^{2}}ds

(183)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\|\bm{M}\|^{2}+36(\tau^{*})^{2}dr+4(\tau^{*})^{2}d\int_{C\sqrt{d}}^{\infty}s^{2}e^{-cs^{2}}ds

(184)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\|\bm{M}\|^{2}+36(\tau^{*})^{2}dr+4(\tau^{*})^{2}C^{\prime}d^{2}\exp(-cd^{2})

(185)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\|\bm{M}\|^{2}+36(\tau^{*})^{2}dr

(186)

\displaystyle\leq 4r\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\min}(\bm{M})^{2}+36(\tau^{*})^{2}dr,

(187)

provided
d
is sufficiently large, where we have used the identity
\mathbb{E}X=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(X>s\big)ds

for positive random variable
X
.
Therefore, we have that
\tau^{*}
satisfies

\displaystyle(\tau^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}+\frac{4r\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\min}(\bm{M})^{2}}{n}+\frac{4\cdot 36(\tau^{*})^{2}dr}{n},

(188)

which, after rearranging yields

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{4\cdot 36dr}{n}\bigg)(\tau^{*})^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}+\frac{4\kappa^{2}dr^{2}}{n}\frac{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{M})^{2}}{dr}\leq\sigma^{2}+\frac{4\kappa^{2}dr^{2}}{n}\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{r}.

(189)

Recalling that
n\geq C_{2}dr^{2}
by
2
shows that

\displaystyle(\tau^{*})^{2}\leq 2\bigg(\sigma^{2}+\frac{4\kappa^{2}dr^{2}}{n}\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{r}\bigg)\leq 6\kappa^{2}\sigma^{2},

(190)

where we have used that
\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}r}\leq C_{2}
by
1
and
n\geq C_{3}dr^{2}
for some large constant
C_{3}
by
2
.
This same argument shows that

\displaystyle\bigg|(\tau^{*})^{2}-\sigma^{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}

(191)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4r\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\min}(\bm{M})^{2}}{n}+\frac{4\cdot 36(\tau^{*})^{2}dr}{n}

(192)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4dr\kappa^{2}\lambda_{\min}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}nr}+\frac{24\cdot 36\kappa^{2}\sigma^{2}dr}{n}

(193)

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\sigma^{2}\frac{dr}{n}\bigg),

(194)

where we have used the bound
\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}r}\leq C_{2}
by
1
.
It remains to bound
|\zeta^{*}-1|
. We have

\displaystyle\bigg|\zeta^{*}-1\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}^{*}\rangle}{n\tau^{*}}

(195)

Suppose that
\bm{Z}^{*}=\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})=\bm{\widehat{U}}\bm{\widehat{\Lambda}}\bm{\widehat{U}}^{\top}
. Then

\displaystyle\langle\bm{H},\bm{Z}^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{\widehat{U}}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{\widehat{U}},\bm{\widehat{\Lambda}}\rangle\leq\|\bm{\widehat{U}}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{\widehat{U}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\widehat{\Lambda}}\|_{F}

(196)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*}))\|\bm{H}\|\|\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}\|

(197)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*}))\bigg(\tau^{*}\|\bm{H}\|^{2}+\|\bm{H}\|\|\bm{M}\|\bigg).

(198)

Plugging this in above yields

\displaystyle\big|\zeta^{*}-1\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n\tau^{*}}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*}))\bigg(\tau^{*}\|\bm{H}\|^{2}+\|\bm{H}\|\|\bm{M}\|\bigg)\bigg\}

(199)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\|\bm{H}\|^{2}\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})\bigg\}+\frac{\lambda_{r}\sqrt{d}}{n\tau^{*}}\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\|\bm{H}\|\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})\bigg\}.

(200)

Our previous argument has already shown that there is a universal constant
C>0
such that as long as
d\geq C
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\|\bm{H}\|^{2}\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})\bigg\}\leq Cdr.

(201)

A similar argument shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\|\bm{H}\|\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ST}}(\tau^{*})\bigg\}\leq C\sqrt{d}r.

(202)

As a result, by
1
we have that

\displaystyle\big|\zeta^{*}-1\big|\leq C\frac{dr}{n}\bigg(1+\frac{\lambda_{r}}{\tau^{*}}\bigg)\leq C\frac{dr}{n}\frac{\lambda_{r}}{\sigma}\leq CC_{2}\frac{dr^{3/2}}{n}=O\bigg(\frac{dr^{3/2}}{n}\bigg).

(203)

∎

A.3.2
Proof of
Lemma

11

Proof of
Lemma

11
.

We mimic the proof in
Celentano et al. 2023
.
First we observe that for
\zeta^{*},\tau^{*}
fixed, the function
\bm{H}\mapsto\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})
is a proximal operator, and hence is
\tau^{*}
-Lipschitz. Therefore, the random variable
\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}/\sqrt{dr}
is
\frac{(\tau^{*})^{2}}{dr}-
subgaussian. In addition, by
Lemma

4
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}=(\tau^{*})^{2}-\sigma^{2}=O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma^{2}\bigg),

(204)

where the last bound comes from
Lemma

6
. Therefore one has

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}}{dr}

\displaystyle=O(1).

(205)

Consequently, we see that

\displaystyle\bm{H}\mapsto\frac{1}{dr}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

(206)

is
(\frac{C_{\sigma}}{dr},\frac{C_{\sigma}}{dr})
sub-Gamma by Proposition G.5 of
Miolane and Montanari 2021
. Therefore, by tail bounds on sub-Gamma random variables,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg|>sdr\bigg\}\leq C\exp(-cdr(s\vee s^{2})),

(207)

which is what was required to show.

For the other assertion, we first observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\mathrm{rank}(\bm{Z}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})\leq r\bigg\}=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}\big(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}\big)|\leq\lambda/\zeta^{*}\bigg\}.

(208)

By
Lemma

6
it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\zeta^{*}}=\frac{\lambda}{1+O(\frac{dr^{3/2}}{n})}\geq\lambda/2.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}\big(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}\big)|\leq\lambda/\zeta^{*}\bigg\}\geq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{|\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}\big(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}\big)|\leq\lambda/2\bigg\}.

(209)

By Weyl’s inequality,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}\big(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}\big)|\leq\tau^{*}\|\bm{H}\|.

(210)

Since
\bm{H}
is a
{\sf GOE}(d)
random matrix, it holds that
\|\bm{H}\|\leq 32\sqrt{d}
with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
for
d\geq C
. In addition, by
Lemma

6
it holds that
(\tau^{*})^{2}\leq C\sigma^{2}
, where
C
depends only on
\kappa
. Consequently, as long as
\lambda\geq C_{3}\sigma\sqrt{d}
, for
C_{3}
larger than some constant (possibly depending on
\kappa
), it holds that
\widehat{\lambda}_{r+1}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})\leq\tau^{*}\|\bm{H}\|\leq C\sigma\sqrt{d}\leq\lambda/2
. The final assertion holds by the same logic and the triangle inequality.
∎

A.3.3
Informal Derivation of Fixed-Point Equations

We now give more details on the heuristic derivation of
(\tau^{*},\zeta^{*})
in
Section

3.2
.
Recall that our goal is to study
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}
defined via

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}=\argmin_{\bm{Z}}\frac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}(\bm{Z-M})-\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{F}^{2}+\lambda\|\bm{Z}\|_{*}.

(211)

Define the reparameterized cost function:

\displaystyle C^{(n)}_{\lambda}(\bm{W}):=\frac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}(\bm{W})-\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{F}^{2}+\lambda\big\{\|\bm{W+M}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}.

(212)

It is immediately evident that if
\bm{W}
minimizes
C_{\lambda}^{(n)}
over the set of matrices
\bm{W}
such that
\bm{W}+\bm{M}
is positive semidefinite, then
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}=\bm{W}+\bm{M}
.

Observe that we can equivalently write

\displaystyle C_{\lambda}^{(n)}(\bm{W})

\displaystyle=\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\langle\mathcal{X}[\bm{W}],\bm{\nu}\rangle-\langle\bm{\varepsilon},\bm{\nu}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\nu}\|^{2}+\lambda\big\{\|\bm{W+M}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}.

(213)

Consequently, the min-max above takes the form required for the Matrix CGMT. Therefore,
we can show that the minimum value of
C_{\lambda}^{(n)}
is related to that of the function
L_{\lambda}^{(n)}
, defined via

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}^{(n)}(\mathcal{W}):=\frac{1}{dr}\bigg(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{W}\in\mathcal{W}\\
\bm{W}+\bm{M}\succcurlyeq 0\end{subarray}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle/\sqrt{n}+

\displaystyle\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-\langle\bm{\varepsilon},\bm{\nu}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\nu}\|^{2}

(214)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\lambda(\|\bm{W}+\bm{M}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*})\bigg).

(215)

Again, the argument of
L_{\lambda}^{(n)}
is a set (the set for which
\bm{W}
is minimized over).
Therefore, it suffices to analyze the minimum value of
L_{\lambda}^{(n)}
over
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
. By expanding
L_{\lambda}^{(n)}
out further and manipulating, we can show that

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}^{(n)}(\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})

\displaystyle=\max_{\beta>0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\tau}+\tau\bigg)\frac{\|\bm{g}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\gamma_{n}\beta}{2}-\gamma_{n}\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}

(216a)

\displaystyle\quad+\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\\
\bm{W}+\bm{M}\succcurlyeq 0\end{subarray}}\bigg\{\beta\frac{\|\bm{W}\|_{F}^{2}}{2dr\tau}+\beta\frac{\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle}{dr}+\frac{\lambda}{dr}\big\{\|\bm{W}+\bm{M}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}\bigg\}.

(216b)

After changing variables back to
\bm{Z}=\bm{W}+\bm{M}
, the inner minimization can be obtained by soft-thresholding
\bm{M}+\tau\bm{H}
at level
\lambda/\zeta
where
\zeta=\tau\beta
.

Proof of
Lemmas

7
and
8

Proof of
Lemmas

7
and
8
.

We prove the result first for hard thresholding.
First, define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle:=\bigg\{\|\bm{H}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\bigg\}.

(217)

We observe that on
\mathcal{E}
, since
\lambda\geq C_{3}\sigma\sqrt{d}
and
\tau^{*}\leq C\sigma
by
Lemma

6
, it holds that
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}
is rank
r
. Consequently, on this event,

\displaystyle\phi\bigg(\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\phi\bigg(\mathcal{P}_{{\sf rank}-r}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})\bigg)

(218)

\displaystyle=\phi\bigg(\mathcal{P}_{{\sf rank}-r}\bigg[\bm{M}+\sigma\bigg(1+O\big(\frac{dr}{n}\big)\bigg)\bm{H}\bigg]\bigg).

(219)

Let
\bm{U}_{\sigma}\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}
denote the leading rank
r
approximation of
\bm{M}+\sigma\bm{H}
, and let
\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{\top}
be the leading rank
r
approximation of
\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}
. Recall that we let
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\sigma},\bm{U}_{\tau}}
denote the Frobenius-optimal orthogonal matrix aligning
\bm{U}_{\sigma}
to
\bm{U}_{\tau}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{U}_{\sigma}\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{U}_{\sigma}\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\big(\bm{U}_{\sigma}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}\big)^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\bm{U}_{\sigma}\big[\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}^{\top}-\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\big]\bm{U}_{\tau}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\big[\bm{U}_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\big]\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\|\bm{U}_{\sigma}\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\big(\bm{U}_{\sigma}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}\big)^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\big[\bm{U}_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\big]\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{\top}\|_{F}}_{=:T_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\big[\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}\big]\|_{F}+\|\big[\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}-\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\sigma},\bm{U}_{\tau}}\big]\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\|_{F}}_{=:T_{2}}+\underbrace{\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}-\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\|_{F}}_{=:T_{3}}.

We will analyze each term in turn. First, we note that on the event
\mathcal{E}
it holds that

\displaystyle\max\big\{|\lambda_{r}(\bm{M}+\sigma\bm{H})-\lambda_{r+1}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})|,|\lambda_{r+1}(\bm{M}+\sigma\bm{H})-\lambda_{r}(\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H})|\big\}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{d}\leq\lambda_{\min}(\bm{M}).

Consequently, the Davis-Kahan Theorem implies

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\sigma}\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{r}\|\bm{U}_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\sigma}\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\|\leq C\frac{|\tau^{*}-\sigma|\sqrt{dr}}{\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}.

We also note that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\sigma}\bm{U}_{\tau}}-\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}\|\leq 1-\|\cos\Theta(\bm{U}_{\sigma},\bm{U}_{\tau})\|\leq\|\sin\Theta(\bm{U}_{\sigma},\bm{U}_{\tau})\|^{2}\leq C^{2}\frac{|\tau^{*}-\sigma|^{2}dr}{\lambda_{r}^{2}(\bm{M})},

which follows from the fact that
\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\sigma},\bm{U}_{\tau}}
is computed from the SVD of
\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}
, and the singular values of
\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}
are equal to the cosines of the principal angles between the subspaces. We now anlyze each term.

•

The term
T_{1}
.
We have that

\displaystyle\|\big[\bm{U}_{\sigma}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{\tau}\big]\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\bm{U}_{\tau}^{\top}\|_{F}\leq\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\|\frac{C|\tau^{*}-\sigma|\sqrt{dr}}{\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\lesssim|\tau^{*}-\sigma|\sqrt{dr}.

The same bound holds for the other term.

•

The term
T_{2}
.
We have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\big[\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U}_{\tau},\bm{U}_{\sigma}}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}\big]\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\|C^{2}\frac{|\tau^{*}-\sigma|^{2}dr}{\lambda_{r}^{2}(\bm{M})}\leq C^{\prime}|\tau^{*}-\sigma|^{2}\sqrt{dr}\frac{\sqrt{dr}}{\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})},

(220)

which follows by
1
. A similar bound holds for the other quantity.

•

The term
T_{3}
.
We observe that

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Lambda}_{\sigma}\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}-\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{U}_{\tau}\bm{\Lambda}_{\tau}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{U}_{\sigma}^{\top}\big[\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}-(\bm{M}+\sigma\bm{H})\big]\bm{U}_{\tau}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{rd}|\tau^{*}-\sigma|.

Combining these results, we have that on the event
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}\|_{F}\leq C|\tau^{*}-\sigma|\sqrt{dr}+C\sqrt{dr}|\tau^{*}-\sigma|^{2}.

By
Lemma

6
it holds that

\displaystyle|\tau^{*}-\sigma|\leq\frac{|(\tau^{*})^{2}-\sigma^{2}|}{\tau^{*}+\sigma}

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma\bigg).

Therefore, we have that on the event
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma\bigg).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)-\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)\bigg\}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}^{(\lambda)}-\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}\|_{F}

(221)

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma\bigg)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\|\bm{M}+\tau^{*}\bm{H}\|_{F}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{E}^{c}}+\mathbb{E}\|\bm{M}+\sigma^{*}\bm{H}\|_{F}\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{E}^{c}}\bigg)

(222)

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma\bigg)+\frac{\|\bm{M}\|}{\sqrt{dr}}\mathbb{P}\{\mathcal{E}^{c}\}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{H}\|\mathbb{I}_{\mathcal{E}^{c}}

(223)

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma\bigg)+C\exp(-cd)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\int_{C\sqrt{d}}^{\infty}\exp(-cs^{2})ds

(224)

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma\bigg).

(225)

Here we have used the fact that
\mathbb{P}\{\|\bm{H}\|\geq C\sqrt{d}+s\}\leq 2\exp(-cs^{2})
by standard tail bounds for norms of random matrices
(
Bandeira and Handel 2016
)
.

We now prove the result for soft thresholding. First, we note that the exact same argument continues applies if we
make the replacement
\bm{M}\mapsto\bm{M}-\lambda\bm{I}
. Consequently, we have that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*})}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)\bigg|

(226)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*})}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)\bigg|

(227)

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\frac{dr}{n}\sigma\bigg)+\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*})}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)\bigg|.

(228)

Therefore, it suffices to focus on the second term.

On the event
\mathcal{E}
the quantity
\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})
is simply hard thresholding of
\bm{M}-\lambda+\tau^{*}\bm{H}
at level
\lambda/\zeta^{*}
, and similarly for
\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*})
. Therefore, both matrices share the same eigenvectors, and hence on the event
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})-\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\tau^{*})\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2r}\lambda\bigg(\frac{1}{\zeta^{*}}-1\bigg)=O\bigg(\sigma\sqrt{rd}\times\frac{dr^{3/2}}{n}\bigg),

where we have used the fact that
|\zeta^{*}-1|=O\bigg(\frac{dr^{3/2}}{n}\bigg)
by
Lemma

6
. Therefore, the same argument as for
\bm{Z}_{{\sf HT}}
shows that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda/\zeta^{*})}(\tau^{*})}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)-\mathbb{E}\phi\bigg(\frac{\bm{Z}_{{\sf ST}}^{(\lambda)}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{dr}}\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\frac{dr^{3/2}}{n}\bigg).

(229)

∎

Proof of the Matrix CGMT (
Theorem

3
)

In this section we prove the Matrix CGMT. Our proof is based on
Miolane and Montanari 2021
.

Proof of
Theorem

3
.

Define the Gaussian processes

\displaystyle X(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})

\displaystyle:=\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle+\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle;

(230)

\displaystyle Y(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})

\displaystyle:=\langle\mathcal{A}[\bm{W}],\bm{\nu}\rangle+\|\bm{\nu}\|\|\bm{W}\|z,

(231)

where
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
is independent from
\mathcal{A}(\cdot)
,
\bm{g}
is a standard Gaussian vector, and
\bm{H}
is a
{\sf GOE}(d)
random matrix. Observe that since
\bm{H}
is
{\sf GOE}(d)
,
\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\|\bm{W}\|_{F}^{2})
for symmetric
\bm{W}
, whence we need only define the processes for symmetric matrices
\bm{W}
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[Y(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})Y(\bm{W}^{\prime},\bm{\nu^{\prime}})\bigg]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\bigg[X(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})X(\bm{W}^{\prime},\bm{\nu^{\prime}})\bigg]

(232)

\displaystyle=\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\|\bm{\nu}\|\|\bm{W}^{\prime}\|_{F}\|\bm{\nu}^{\prime}\|+\langle\bm{\nu},\bm{\nu}^{\prime}\rangle\langle\bm{W},\bm{W}^{\prime}\rangle-\|\bm{\nu}\|\|\bm{\nu}^{\prime}\|\langle\bm{W},\bm{W}^{\prime}\rangle-\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\|\bm{W}^{\prime}\|_{F}\langle\bm{\nu},\bm{\nu}^{\prime}\rangle

(233)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\|\bm{W}^{\prime}\|_{F}-\langle\bm{W},\bm{W}^{\prime}\rangle\bigg)\bigg(\|\bm{\nu}\|\|\bm{\nu}^{\prime}\|-\langle\bm{\nu},\bm{\nu}^{\prime}\rangle\bigg)\geq 0,

(234)

with equality when
\bm{W}=\bm{W}^{\prime}
or
\bm{\nu}=\bm{\nu}^{\prime}
. Therefore, by Theorem G.2 of
Miolane and Montanari 2021
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\nu}}Y(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq t\bigg\}\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\nu}}X(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq t\bigg\}.

(235)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}Y(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq t\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}Y(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq t|z\leq 0\bigg\}

(236)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}\langle\mathcal{A}(\bm{W}),\bm{\nu}\rangle+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq t|z\leq 0\bigg\}

(237)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\Phi(\mathcal{A})\leq t\bigg\}.

(238)

Rewriting the equation above yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\Phi(\mathcal{A})\leq t\bigg\}\leq 2\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}X(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq t\bigg\}=2\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\phi(\bm{g},\bm{H})\leq t\bigg\}.

(239)

This proves the first result.

Now suppose that
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}
are convex and that
\psi
is convex-concave. Note that the argument above relies not on the minimization over
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}
and maximization over
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}
– we are free to minimize instead over
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}
and maximize over
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}
. Then this argument above shows that (using
-\psi
instead of
\psi
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}\max_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\langle\mathcal{A}(\bm{W}),\bm{\nu}\rangle-\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq-t\bigg\}\leq 2\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\min_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}\max_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle+\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle-\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq-t\bigg\}.

(240)

Multiplying everything by
-1
and using the fact that
\mathcal{A}(\cdot)
,
\bm{H}
, and
\bm{g}
are equal in distribution to
-\mathcal{A}(\cdot)
,
-\bm{H}
and
-\bm{g}
respectively yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{W}),\bm{\nu}\rangle+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\geq t\bigg\}\leq 2\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle+\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\geq t\bigg\}.

(241)

We observe that since
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}
are both convex and
\psi
is convex-concave, by Sion’s minimax theorem we are able to switch the minimum and maximum on the left hand side. For the right hand side we need not switch, but instead only observe that

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle+\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu})\leq\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{W}}}\max_{\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{S}_{\bm{\nu}}}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle+\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle+\psi(\bm{W},\bm{\nu}).

(242)

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Lemmas in
Section

2
(Theorem
2
)

Throughout this section we fix a constant
\delta_{2r}=\frac{c_{0}}{\sqrt{r}}
, which we are able to do on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
by
2
and
Lemma

1
. Throughout this section we denote
\bm{U}^{*}
as any matrix such that
\bm{U}^{*}\bm{U}^{*\top}=\bm{M}
.

Proof of
Lemma

3

In this subsection we prove
Lemma

3
. The proof is based on studying the second derivative of
f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}
under the necessary conditions for local minima. First, the following lemma provides a calculation of the Hessian of
f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}
.

Lemma 12
.

Let
\Delta=\bm{U}-\bm{U}_{2}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{2}}
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]

\displaystyle=.5\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top})\rangle

(243)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\langle\nabla f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}),\Delta\rangle+\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\rangle-4\lambda\langle\bm{U},\bm{\Delta}\rangle

(244)

\displaystyle\qquad-1.5\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M})\rangle

(245)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta\rangle}]+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}.

(246)

Proof.

See
Section

B.2
.
∎

With this calculation at hand, we may prove
Lemma

3
.

Proof of
Lemma

3
.

First we will show that
\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]
is upper bounded when
\bm{U}
is a critical point of
f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}
. The proof is motivated by
Ge et al. 2017
.

First, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf Good}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\|\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F};

(247)

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\|\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}

(248)

By the restricted isometry property (e.g. Lemma 3 of
Chi et al. 2019
), we have that

\displaystyle\bigg|\big\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M})\big\rangle-\langle\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M},\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\rangle\bigg|\leq\delta_{2r}\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

(249)

and

\displaystyle\bigg|\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top})\rangle-\langle\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top},\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\rangle\bigg|\leq\delta_{2r}\|\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}

(250)

Consequently, if
\bm{U}
satisfies
\nabla f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})=0
,

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]

\displaystyle\leq.5\big(1+\delta_{2r})\|\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}

(251)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\big(\|\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}\big)

(252)

\displaystyle\quad-1.5(1-\delta_{2r})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

(253)

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}-4\lambda\langle\bm{U,\Delta}\rangle.

(254)

Next, we note that by Lemma 6 of
Ge et al. 2017
, if
\bm{\Delta}=\bm{U}^{*}-\bm{U}\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U},\bm{U}^{*}}
and
\bm{U}^{*}\bm{U}^{*\top}=\bm{M}
, then

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2};\qquad\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}.

(255)

Plugging in these bounds shows that

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+\delta_{2r})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

(256)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\big(\|\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\big)

(257)

\displaystyle\quad-1.5(1+\delta_{2r})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

(258)

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\frac{1}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}-4\lambda\langle\bm{U,\Delta}\rangle

(259)

\displaystyle=\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg(-.5+.5\delta_{2r}+\frac{\lambda}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\bigg)

(260)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\bigg(\|\bm{U}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\bigg)

(261)

\displaystyle\qquad-4\lambda\langle\bm{U,\Delta}\rangle.

(262)

We further have that

\displaystyle\|\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\bm{U},\bm{U}^{*}})^{\top})\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\bm{U}^{*}\|\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}

(263)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\bm{U}^{*}\|\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}

(264)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{1}(\bm{M})}{\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2(\sqrt{2}-1)}}\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}

(265)

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{\kappa}+\sqrt{2})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F},

(266)

where the same bound holds for
\langle\bm{U},\bm{\Delta}\rangle
. Consequently, plugging this in, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]

\displaystyle\leq\big(1+\delta_{2r})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

(267)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\big(\|\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\|_{F}+\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\big)

(268)

\displaystyle\quad-1.5(1+\delta_{2r})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

(269)

\displaystyle\qquad+\lambda\frac{1}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}-4\lambda\langle\bm{U,\Delta}\rangle

(270)

\displaystyle=\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg(-.5+.5\delta_{2r}+\frac{\lambda}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\bigg)

(271)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\sigma\sqrt{dr}\bigg((\sqrt{\kappa}+\sqrt{2})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}+\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\bigg)

(272)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\lambda(\sqrt{\kappa}+\sqrt{2})\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}

(273)

\displaystyle=\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}\bigg(-.5+.5\delta_{2r}+\frac{\lambda}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\bigg)

(274)

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\bigg(C\sigma\sqrt{\kappa dr}+4\lambda\sqrt{\kappa}\bigg).

(275)

Under the assumption that
\delta_{2r}\leq\frac{1}{10}
,
\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})\geq C_{1}\sigma\sqrt{dr}
, and
C_{3}\sigma\sqrt{d}\leq\lambda\leq C_{4}\sigma\sqrt{d}
, it holds that

\displaystyle-.5+.5\delta_{2r}+\frac{\lambda}{2(\sqrt{2}-1)\lambda_{r}(\bm{M})}\leq-.1.

(276)

If
\bm{U}
is a local minimum, then it holds that
0\leq\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]
.
Rearranging this yields

\displaystyle.1\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\bigg(C\sigma\sqrt{\kappa dr}+4\lambda\sqrt{\kappa}\bigg).

(277)

Note that if
\bm{UU}^{\top}=\bm{M}
then the result is trivially proven, so without loss of generality we may assume
\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}
is positive. Rearranging shows that we must have

\displaystyle\|\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}\|_{F}\leq 10C\sigma\sqrt{\kappa dr}+40\lambda\sqrt{\kappa},

(278)

which completes the proof, since
\kappa=O(1)
by
1
and
\lambda\leq C_{5}\sigma\sqrt{d}
by
3
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

12

Proof of
Lemma

12
.

First, we have that

\displaystyle f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[y_{i}-\langle\bm{UU}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle/\sqrt{n}\bigg]^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}\|\bm{U}\|_{F}^{2},

(279)

whence

\displaystyle\nabla f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\langle\bm{UU}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-y_{i}\bigg]\bm{X}_{i}\bm{U}+\lambda\bm{U}.

(280)

As a result,

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}

\displaystyle f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]

(281)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\langle\nabla f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}+t\bm{\Delta}),\bm{\Delta}\rangle\bigg|_{t=0}

(282)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\bigg\langle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\langle\bm{(U+t\bm{\Delta})(U+t\Delta)}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-y_{i}\bigg]\bm{X}_{i}(\bm{U}+t\bm{\Delta})+\lambda(\bm{U}+t\bm{\Delta}),\bm{\Delta}\bigg\rangle\bigg|_{t=0}

(283)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\langle\bm{\Delta U}^{\top}+\bm{U\Delta}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\bigg\rangle\bigg\langle\bm{X}_{i}\bm{U},\bm{\Delta}\bigg\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\langle\bm{UU}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-y_{i}\bigg]\langle\bm{X}_{i}\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}\rangle+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}

(284)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\langle\bm{U\Delta}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\bigg\rangle\bigg\langle\bm{X}_{i}\bm{U},\bm{\Delta}\bigg\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\langle\bm{UU}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-y_{i}\bigg]\langle\bm{X}_{i}\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}\rangle+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}

(285)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\langle\bm{U\Delta}^{\top},\bm{X}_{i}\bigg\rangle^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\langle\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M},\bm{X}_{i}\rangle\bigg]\langle\bm{X}_{i}\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}\rangle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i}\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}\rangle\bigg]+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}

(286)

\displaystyle=.5\big\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta U}^{\top}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta U}^{\top})\big\rangle

(287)

\displaystyle\qquad+\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top})\rangle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i}\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}\rangle\bigg]+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}.

(288)

Next, we note that

\displaystyle\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}+\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}=\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta U}^{\top}.

(289)

Plugging this in yields

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]

\displaystyle=.5\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top})\rangle

(290)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}+\bm{\Delta U}^{\top})\rangle

(291)

\displaystyle\qquad-1.5\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M})\rangle

(292)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta\rangle}]+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}.

(293)

In addition, we have that

\displaystyle\langle\nabla f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}),\Delta\rangle

\displaystyle=.5\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{U}\Delta^{\top}+\bm{\Delta U}^{\top})\rangle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\rangle+\lambda\langle\bm{U},\bm{\Delta}\rangle,

(294)

whence

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U})[\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta}]

\displaystyle=.5\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{\Delta\Delta}^{\top})\rangle

(295)

\displaystyle\qquad+4\langle\nabla f^{(\lambda)}_{{\sf ncvx}}(\bm{U}),\Delta\rangle+\frac{4}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{U\Delta}^{\top}\rangle-4\lambda\langle\bm{U},\bm{\Delta}\rangle

(296)

\displaystyle\qquad-1.5\langle\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M}),\mathcal{X}(\bm{UU}^{\top}-\bm{M})\rangle

(297)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\varepsilon_{i}\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{\Delta},\bm{\Delta\rangle}]+\lambda\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{F}^{2}.

(298)

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
4

In this section we study the convex estimator
\bm{Z}^{(\lambda)}
defined via

\displaystyle\argmin_{\bm{Z}}\frac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}(\bm{Z-M})-\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{F}^{2}+\lambda\|\bm{Z}\|_{*}.

(299)

Recall we denote
\mathcal{X}(\cdot)
as the operator such that
\mathcal{X}(\bm{M})=\langle\bm{X}_{i},\bm{M}\rangle/\sqrt{n}
. Define

\displaystyle C^{(n)}_{\lambda}(\bm{W}):=\frac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{X}(\bm{W})-\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{F}^{2}+\lambda\big\{\|\bm{Z}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}.

(300)

Define also

\displaystyle\psi^{(n)}_{\lambda}(\bm{W},\bm{\nu}):=\frac{1}{dr}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-\frac{1}{dr}\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-\frac{1}{dr}\langle\bm{\varepsilon},\bm{\nu}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{dr}\|\bm{\nu}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{dr}\lambda\big\{\|\bm{W}+\bm{M}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}.

(301)

We define, for a set
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,

\displaystyle L^{(n)}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{D}):=\min_{\bm{W}\in\mathcal{D}:\bm{W}+\bm{M}\succcurlyeq 0}\max_{\bm{\nu}}\psi^{(n)}_{\lambda}(\bm{W},\bm{\nu}).

(302)

In essence,
L_{\lambda}^{(n)}(\mathcal{D})
denotes the minimizer of
L_{\lambda}(\cdot)
over the set
\mathcal{D}
. By slight abuse of notation, we let
L_{\lambda}^{(n)}(\bm{W}):=L_{\lambda}^{(n)}(\{\bm{W}\})
(equivalently, the minimizer over the singleton set
\{\bm{W}\}
).
We observe that

\displaystyle dr

\displaystyle\times L_{\lambda}^{(n)}(\mathcal{W})

(303)

\displaystyle=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{W}\in\mathcal{W}\\
\bm{W}+\bm{M}\succcurlyeq 0\end{subarray}}\max_{\bm{\nu}}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-\|\bm{\nu}\|\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-\langle\bm{\varepsilon},\bm{\nu}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{\nu}\|^{2}+\lambda\big\{\|\bm{W}+\bm{M}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}

(304)

\displaystyle=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{W}\in\mathcal{W}\\
\bm{W}+\bm{M}\succcurlyeq 0\end{subarray}}\max_{\beta>0}\max_{\|\bm{\nu}\|=\beta}\|\bm{W}\|_{F}\langle\bm{g},\bm{\nu}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-\beta\langle\bm{H},\bm{W}\rangle/\sqrt{n}-\langle\bm{\varepsilon},\bm{\nu}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\beta^{2}+\lambda\big\{\|\bm{W}+\bm{M}\|_{*}-\|\bm{M}\|_{*}\big\}

(305)

\displaystyle=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{W}\in\mathcal{W}\\
